# `002-390-X` Risky Parse Model v1

Date: 2026-05-31

This is intentionally below the accepted bar. The point is to make falsifiable bets that can be broken.

## Core Parse Bet

Candidate:

`L ... 002 390 X` is a governed branch construction. Most semantics are demoted below candidate; the carried rule is mainly syntactic:

- `L` = left stem or qualifier
- `002` = parser frame cue; relational / dependency-opener value is still wild shot
- `390` = open class head, probably an authority, office, or holder-class head
- `X` = selector suffix naming branch mechanics: continuing/open, terminal/closed, one-complement linker, or inherited local formula bridge

Ugly working gloss:

`L-002-390-X` = "L in/under/for the 390-class, subtype X"

This is not a language identity claim or phonetic reading. It is a translation-system rule candidate: parse `002-390-X` as a small governed phrase, not as three independent signs.

Left-context update:

`L` is no longer just uninterpreted stem material. The current risky parser treats selected left signs as operator-like context:

| left context | live function bet |
|---|---|
| final `235` before `002` | rank/title trigger only when the governed head is P086-family `390/405` |
| `032` before `002` without `235` | boundary/formula/result context; should avoid `125` rank tails |
| final `004` before `002` | neutral qualifier that can split between terminal `095` closure and `125` rank continuation |

Shadow caution:

The left-context model is not shadow-promoted. Exact-left duplicate groups are sparse. `004` has exact-shadow support, and `032` without `235` has one exact no-`125` shadow, but the `235` rank trigger only has suffix-level shadow support. Keep `235` as a candidate from aggregate P086 behavior, not from exact-left minimal pairs.

Scope caution:

`032` without `235` is not a global no-`125` sign value. The broad governed-frame rule breaks on `H-130: 806-002-595-812-125-717`. The narrower current rule is: inside P086/target `002-390`, `032` without `235` suppresses `125` and biases toward formula/result branches.

## Why This Is A Candidate

The local corpus makes `390` unusual after `002`.

Most common heads after `002` are closures:

| head after `002` | n | main behavior |
|---|---:|---|
| `861` | 174 | mostly terminal |
| `817` | 156 | overwhelmingly terminal |
| `820` | 102 | overwhelmingly terminal |
| `405` | 32 | copied Harappa tablet formula, mostly `405-501` |
| `390` | 15 | high-diversity `X` slot |

`002-390` has 15 rows and 10 next signs. That is the key risky fact. It behaves less like a closure and more like a branch head.

## Competing Models

Candidate A, grammar:

`002` opens a dependency, `390` is the governed head, and `X` is a selector. Repeated `X` values are meaningful classes or role tags.

Candidate B, copied visual register:

`002-390-X` is an artifact of copied seal/tablet visual formulae. Repeated `X` values are not grammar; they are inherited chunks.

Current lean: Candidate A is ahead, but not promoted.

Reason: `002-405-X` is what a copied visual-register template looks like here: 29/32 rows are the same Harappa `+520-240-002-405-501+` tablet formula. `002-390-X` is not like that. It has mixed sites, objects, prefixes, and selector tails.

## Sign Function Bets

### Bet 1: `002` Is A Dependency Opener

Confidence: candidate as a frame cue; wild shot as actual dependency/relational morphology

Function bet: `002` may mean something like REL / governed-by / assigned-to. The safer parser bet is weaker: `002` tells the parser to score the following sign as a frame head before reading its tail.

Prediction:

Rows with `002` followed by terminal heads (`817`, `820`, `861`) close phrases. Rows with `002` followed by open heads (`390`, sometimes `031`, `368`, `220`) create selector tails.

Kill condition:

If matched left stems show the same head/tail behavior without `002`, this collapses into ordinary adjacency.

Counterfactual damage:

A direct `390` contrast damaged the strong form. Canonical post-`002` `390` has `15` hits and `10` next signs, but non-`002` `390` is also diverse: `212` hits and `61` next signs. So `002` cannot yet be claimed as proven dependency morphology from `390` contrast alone. It remains a frame cue in the parser and a wild-shot relational sign value.

### Bet 2: `390` Under `002` Is An Open Class Head

Confidence: candidate

Function bet: `390` contributes a class-head meaning, probably authority/office/holder class rather than commodity or numeral.

Why not just raw `390`?

Raw `390` is dominated by `390-590`:

`390-590` occurs 73 times; only one of those is after `002`.

So `002-390-X` is not simply the ordinary `390-590` formula. `002` changes what `390` can take.

Kill condition:

If source-visible rows show `002-390-X` is usually a copied visual band with the same layout/register and no independent selector behavior, demote to copied formula.

Narrowing after open-head classifier:

`390` is not unique as an open post-`002` head. That stronger claim is dead. The surviving bet is that `390` is a high-count, non-formula-locked member of an open-selector-head class.

Raw-inheritance check:

The branch table is not mostly copied from ordinary raw `390-X` adjacency. A `390-X` inheritance discriminator found `8/9` non-`590` branch values are governed-only or governed-weighted selectors against the non-`002` background: `072`, `095`, `140`, `346`, `530`, `692`, `705`, and `707`. The exceptions matter: `590` is still background `390-590` formula pressure, and `125` is inherited `390-125` pressure that seems refunctionalized by `002` into an open selector.

### Bet 3: `X` Is Mostly A Class/Role Selector, Not A Personal Name

Confidence: candidate

Function bet: the `X` slot is too constrained and too recurrent to be raw personal-name material.

Evidence:

| X | rows in `002-390-X` | provisional role |
|---|---:|---|
| `125` | 4 | open/continuing rank-title selector |
| `095` | 2 | close/terminal administrative-status selector |
| `705` | 2 | close/terminal formal selector; remote/transfer semantics still wild |
| `692` | 1 | close/terminal hard closure selector |
| `590-032` | 1 | open/continuing inherited formula bridge |
| `530-Y` | 1 in `002-390`; 4 across `002-H` | one-complement chained branch; portable candidate only inside governed frames |
| `072`, `346`, `707`, `140` | 1 each | close/terminal singleton selectors |

Sharper branch-table bet after canonical rerun:

`002-390-X` is a branch selector table:

| branch class | signs | prediction |
|---|---|---|
| open/continue | `125`, `530`, `590` | must carry at least one tail sign |
| close/terminal | `072`, `095`, `140`, `346`, `692`, `705`, `707` | must end immediately |

Kill condition:

If held-out rows show the `X` slot behaves like unconstrained personal-name spelling with no repeated selector families, demote. The direct branch-table falsifier is stricter: a source-bound terminal `002-390-125/530/590` or a source-bound continuing `002-390-095/692/705` breaks the current table.

Scope correction after cross-head transfer:

Do not transfer the open/closed table globally. A scan of non-`390` `002-H-X` rows gives only mixed support: `26` non-`390` target-branch frames hit the `002-390` polarity in `19/26` cases. The local `002-390-X` table remains candidate; broad cross-head X polarity is only a wild shot.

Scope correction after raw `390-X` inheritance:

Do not pretend every `X` is equally clean. `590` is a bridge into inherited `390-590-032`. `125` has inherited raw `390-125` pressure, but post-`002` `390-125` is always continuing and has no exact tail overlap with raw non-`002` `390-125`. The branch table survives as candidate because the dirty branches are named and behave differently.

Exception: `530` now has its own candidate sub-rule across governed heads:

`002-H-530-Y`

The current risky read is "H takes/links one complement Y", not a free-standing `530` value.

## Specific Risky Sub-Bets

### `125`: Rank Or Title Selector

Confidence: strengthened candidate for `235-002-(390/405)-...125`; strengthened candidate, not promoted, for the named head+tail carrier parses; wild shot for broad portable `125` sign-level grammar after the random-cell forger; candidate only for tail-conditioned semantic glosses inside specific carrier templates

Bet:

`125` marks a narrow carrier-conditioned rank/title-like continuation slot. `390` selects one use of that slot, but the current evidence is template-bound rather than a portable standalone sign value.

Why:

`390-125` occurs 9 times globally, 4 after `002`. The `002-390-125` rows are all seal rows. The strongest internal pressure is `235-002-branch-125`:

| row | pattern |
|---|---|
| `M-38` | `235-002-390-125-632-032` |
| `M-735` | `235-002-390-125-195` |
| `M-41` | `235-002-405-125-820` |
| `Lh-1` | `235-002-392-590-125` |

Prediction:

Future or held-out `235-002` rows with the P086-family head neighborhood `390/405` should tend to carry `125` in the tail. `235-002-817` and `235-002-861` should usually close without `125`.

New sub-tail bet:

`125` can take portable sub-tails. Two are live:

| sub-tail | current status | implication |
|---|---|---|
| `125-632-032` | candidate sub-tail | occurs under governed `190` and `390`; looks like governed reuse of a transferable terminal compound |
| `125-820` | candidate-edge terminal cap | occurs under governed `390` and `405`; source weight is thin because Sktd-1 is wrapped/non-strict and `820` is broad closure background |

This matters because a personal-name reading should not easily explain the same `125-632-032` tail moving across governed heads. A follow-up formula-pressure test found only `7` total `632-032` rows, `4/7` after `125`, and `3/7` governed after `125`, so this is not just omnipresent filler.

Broader template bet:

`125` is not merely a `390` selector. It appears to participate in governed templates of the shape:

`002-H-(modifier)-125-tail`

Repeated template families now include:

| template | count | sites | current read |
|---|---:|---|---|
| `390-125-632-032` | 2 | Mohenjo-daro | P086/390 compound-tail reuse |
| `610-125-032` | 2 | Harappa, Mohenjo-daro | repeated governed title template |
| `297-350-125-413` | 2 | Bala-kot, Nausharo | repeated governed title template |

The `190-125-632-032` row is still only a wild sibling-head extension: it shares the compound tail, but has only one witness.

Control warning:

Template repetition by itself does not prove grammar. A full governed-template repetition control found `47` repeated `002-H-tail` templates, dominated by closure templates and copy/register controls such as `405-501`. The broader `125` grammar survives only because its repeated families are not exact-text dominated, and because two repeated `125` templates are cross-site. The attack-surface rerun downgrades this from promoted to candidate-mixed, then the random-cell forger demotes broad portable `125` to wild shot: the named carriers survive, but `125` by itself is not rare enough under null pressure.

Raw `390-125` pressure:

`125` is not a clean governed-only selector. It is the one serious inherited-pressure exception in the `002-390-X` table: global `390-125` has `9` canonical rows, `4` after `002` and `5` outside it. The risky parse is now refunctionalization, not invention from zero. Post-`002` `390-125` continues `4/4`; raw non-`002` `390-125` continues only `2/5`, and exact tail overlap between the two lanes is `0`. The `632-032` tail appears in `2/4` post-`002` rows and `0/5` raw non-`002` rows. So `002` may turn inherited `390-125` into an open rank/title selector.

Component bets from the cross-site templates:

| sign/piece | confidence | live function bet |
|---|---|---|
| `610` | candidate | rare governed title head; every current `610` occurrence is `002-610-125-032` |
| `297-350` | candidate as pair | governed head+modifier selecting `125-413`; `297` alone has wider uses |
| `413` | wild outside exact template | terminal title suffix inside `297-350-125-413`, but globally dominated by `575/892` contexts |
| `032` | candidate terminal/pivot, not title-specific | terminal tail after `610-125`, but one non-`610` `125-032` adversarial row exists |

Carrier visual stress:

The `392-590-125` row is not part of the promoted rule. It is a wild extension because the current crosswalk layer does not tie `392` to the P086 family.

### `095`: Administrative Closure Selector

Confidence: candidate mixed for administrative/status selector; candidate only inside `002-390` for terminal closure; name/title reading demoted to wild shot

Bet:

`095` is an administrative/status class. It may mean something like document-status, transaction class, receipt class, account-role class, or result marker. The old stronger wording, generic "closure", is too broad; outside `002-390`, `095` can take continuations.

Rows:

| row | pattern |
|---|---|
| `H-1993` | `004-002-390-095` on a Harappa tablet row |
| `M-71` | `142-002-390-095` on a Mohenjo seal row |

Why:

Canonical `095` has strong tablet/pot/tag/rod/implement and formula pressure, especially around `520` and `595`. The status ecology stress gives `22` canonical occurrences: `13/22` follow `390/520/595`, `14/22` are on tablet/pot/tag/rod/implement rows, `17/22` are outside square/rectangular seal rows, and only `8/22` are globally terminal. In the count-matched baseline plus focus set, `095` ranks `2/65` by `390/520/595` predecessor concentration and `5/65` by tablet/pot/tag/rod/implement share. In `002-390-X`, it is terminal in both checked rows.

Closed-selector subtype update:

`095` is now the best candidate for a status/admin closure subtype among the closed `002-390-X` signs. In the closed-selector ecology discriminator, `095` has `22` canonical occurrences, `14/22` status-like object contexts, and `13/22` predecessors in the `390/520/595` admin frame family. That separates it from `692` and `705`, which close the `002-390` frame for different reasons.

Copy warning:

Raw `095` has `30` occurrences, but the top raw exact string `407-032-520-095-585-017-231` accounts for `8/30`. The administrative/status parse must survive canonical collapse; it cannot lean on raw copy-family inflation.

Prediction:

If H-1993 is source-bound, the side should be short and terminal at `390-095`. If it has a hidden continuation, the terminal-closure wording weakens, but the administrative/status class can survive if the continuation resembles the nonterminal `095` admin trails rather than a name-like tail.

If future `002-390-095` rows cluster as square-seal personal tails with diverse continuations, the name/title reading revives. If they remain short, terminal, and connected to tablet/status ecology, keep status/result as the better parse.

### `705`: Remote / Transfer / Regional Selector

Confidence: source-weak candidate for formal terminal selector under `002-390`; wild shot retained for remote/transfer/regional semantics

Bet:

`705` in `002-390-705` is a terminal selector rather than a personal name. The stronger semantic guess is that it marks a remote, transfer, or regional class, but that semantic step is still only a wild shot.

Rows:

| row | pattern |
|---|---|
| `M-1825` | `031-002-390-705` |
| Dholavira `4237.1` candidate | `388-002-390-705` |

Why:

The two rows are not near-identical formula copies. One is Mohenjo-daro/Lower Indus, one is Dholavira/Gujarat-Kutch; both are square seals, both are terminal in local transcription, and the left stems differ. Globally, canonical `705` occurs `165` times and is usually not after `390`: `390-705` occurs only twice, and both cases are `002-390-705`. That makes the formal branch-slot behavior real enough to test.

The semantic story is still fragile. `705` has heavy global formula pressure: in canonical rows, top previous sign `033` accounts for `78/165` cases, and global `705` is terminal only `38/165`. The `033-705` formula itself has `78` canonical rows and is terminal only `8/78`, with common continuations to `233`, `235`, `240`, `231`, `220`, `002`, and damaged/zero signs. So `002-390-705` is not the common `033-705` formula, but "remote/transfer" is still not candidate-level semantics.

Closed-selector subtype update:

`705` should now be parsed as governed terminal reuse of a larger formula ecology, not as a generic closure suffix. It is globally dominated by `033-705` (`78/165`), and that top predecessor family is usually nonterminal (`8/78` terminal). The `002-390-705` rows are the opposite: terminal, governed-only after `390`, and split across Mohenjo-daro and Dholavira. The semantic gloss "remote/transfer/regional" remains wild because the corpus proves reuse and terminalization, not the external meaning.

Prediction:

If the Dholavira row source-binds, it should preserve terminal `390-705` rather than revealing a longer copied tail. If another sourced `002-390-705` row appears, it should tolerate different left stems but keep short terminal layout. A third terminal `002-390-705` from another region or non-bull administrative context would promote remote/transfer/regional semantics from wild shot to candidate; a source-bound copied `033-705` tail would kill it.

### `530`: One-Complement Chained Branch

Confidence: candidate inside governed `002-H-530-Y` frames; wild shot as a global sign value

Bet:

`530` opens a single complement slot after a governed head. The working parse is:

`002-H-530-Y` = "H linked with complement Y" or "H with subtype Y"

This is not a phonetic value and not a universal `530` meaning. It is a translation-system rule candidate for a narrow frame.

Rows:

| row | pattern | provisional read |
|---|---|---|
| `C-57` | `002-296-530-000` | head `296` takes complement `000` |
| `H-773` | `002-390-530-741` | head `390` takes complement `741` |
| `M-623` | `002-299-530-820` | head `299` takes complement `820` |
| `4182.1` | `002-705-530-904` | head `705` takes complement `904` |

Why:

The `530` one-complement stress found `4/4` governed `002-H-530-Y` frames continue with exactly one following sign, across four heads and four sites: Chanhu-daro, Harappa, Mohenjo-daro, and Dholavira. In count-matched `002-H-X` branch baselines, `530` ranks `1` by one-complement share plus head/site spread.

Adversary:

Global `530` is not clean. It has `18` canonical occurrences, only `13/18` continue, and only `9/18` have exactly one sign after `530`. So this is a governed-frame rule, not a sign meaning. A penultimate-shape forger keeps the rule as candidate but blocks promotion: random four-frame samples match the full `530` shape only `0.00031` of the time, but count-window branch `590` also passes the loose spread threshold if its terminal rows are ignored.

Complement-class add-on:

`530` is now also a candidate selector of closure-like complements, not arbitrary following material. The four complements are `000`, `741`, `820`, and `904`. `000`, `741`, and `820` classify as terminal branch signs in governed `002-H-X` frames; `904` has no governed branch use in the current baseline, but it does classify as a terminal governed head in `002-904-346`. That makes the semantic version narrow: `530` may mean "link H to a closing subtype/output Y", but not yet "link H to any dependent".

Direct-shadow caution:

`530` is not yet a clean nested-layer marker. The direct-branch shadow test found `1/4` same-head shadows: `C-57` has `002-296-530-000`, and `K-146` has a direct `002-296-000`. The other three rows have no direct same-head `002-H-Y` shadow. So `530` can still be a complement linker, but the stronger claim "Y is never directly selected by H" is only a wild/mixed shot.

Prediction:

A new source-visible `002-H-530-Y` row should have exactly one complement after `530`, and that complement should behave as terminal/closure-like elsewhere as a branch or governed head. A terminal `002-H-530`, a productive `002-H-530-Y-Z` continuation, or a new complement that is open/productive in both branch and head roles damages the complement-linker rule. If the four current rows collapse as a visual/register family, demote to wild shot.

### `692`: Hard Terminal Closure Selector

Confidence: candidate narrowed for `002-390-692`; strengthened candidate, not promoted, for portable `060-692` terminal suffix

Bet:

`692` in `M-70 +226-032-002-390-692+` is not a name. It is a terminal closure/result selector selected after the governed `390` head. The stronger revised parse is that `692` is the final element of a portable terminal suffix ecology dominated by `060-692`; `390-692` may be a stripped/borrowed terminal use of that closure element. The suffix is not absolute: `060-692-Y` exceptions exist and must stay in the parser.

Why:

Canonical `692` has `68` occurrences and `50/68` are terminal. Among `23` count-matched signs including `692`, it ranks `1/23` by terminal share and `1/23` by `060` predecessor share. The dominant family is `060-692`: `39/68` canonical `692` occurrences follow `060`, and `33/39` of those are terminal. The direct suffix-collapse stress keeps this from being one copied formula: `060-692` has `39` canonical rows across `5` sites, `4` types, `6` shapes, and `37` exact sequences; top canonical exact-sequence share is only `0.051282`. Outside `060-692`, `692` is still often terminal (`17/29`), but much less cleanly. The local `002-390-692` row is a one-row strict-source terminal branch, so it is a narrow candidate, not a broad semantic acceptance.

Closed-selector subtype update:

The closed-selector ecology discriminator keeps `692` separate from `095` and `705`: it classifies as a portable terminal suffix/result candidate. Its top predecessor is `060` (`39/68`), and that family is terminal `33/39`. So `M-70` should be parsed as `390` selecting a stripped or borrowed terminal-result suffix, not as status/admin `095` and not as formula-reuse `705`.

Prediction:

Future `002-390-692` or close `002-H-692` rows should end at `692` or have only damaged/truncated continuation. A fully preserved long `692-Y-Z` tail under `002-390` would demote this to wild shot. If independent source-visible `060-692` rows preserve terminal behavior across sites/forms, `060-692` becomes the stronger suffix object and `390-692` stays a stripped governed use. If the six continuing Mohenjo-daro square-seal exceptions turn out to be true same-line continuations rather than joins/damage, the parser must treat `060-692` as a strong terminal tendency, not a hard suffix.

### `590-032`: Formula Bridge, Not Ordinary X

Confidence: candidate form, source-blocked; `032` pivot/delimiter is candidate mixed

Bet:

`3335.1` is not just `X = 590`. It embeds the massive `390-590` formula inside the governed `032-002` lane:

`032-002 [390-590-032]`

Why:

`390-590` occurs 73 times globally. Canonical `590-032` occurs `17` times, and `390-590-032` accounts for `7/17`.

`L-139`, `L-196`, `L-198`, `M-1739`, `M-746`, `3335.1`, `M-965`.

Only `3335.1` has `002` directly before that formula. So the risky read is that `3335.1` is a bridge row between the `032-002` governed branch and the older/common `390-590-032` phrase.

The pivot stress sharpened the reason this is not ordinary `X = 590`: among adjacent `590-032` pairs, `next=002` after the final `032` is `3/7` when the pair is part of `390-590-032`, versus `0/10` outside that chunk. Hypergeometric p for that enrichment is `0.051471`. Thin, but just enough to keep the `032` pivot idea alive.

Prediction:

If `3335.1` is source-bound as written, it promotes a rule: `002` can govern an inherited formula chunk, not just a single selector. If `3335.1` dies, the `002-390-X` model loses its best bridge into the common `390-590` formula but not the whole construction.

Sharper hinge bet:

`032` may be a pivot/delimiter sign that can both close the common `390-590-032` formula and open a following `032-002` dependency.

The corpus has seven `390-590-032` rows. Three put `002` immediately after the `032`:

| row | pattern | provisional implication |
|---|---|---|
| `L-139` | `390-590-032-002-880` | `032` closes `390-590` and opens `002` on a Lothal tag |
| `L-196` | `390-590-032-002-000` | same hinge pattern |
| `L-198` | `740-390-590-032-002-000` | same hinge pattern with left `740` |

`3335.1` is the mirror:

`740-205-032-002-390-590-032`

That makes the live bet:

`032-002 [390-590-032]` is a governed re-use of a formula that elsewhere occurs as `[390-590-032] 002`.

Confidence: candidate form if the row source-binds exactly; source-blocked until then. `590` as an ordinary single branch is demoted to wild shot because every target-frame `590` is `590-032`.

`590` is now also a failed adversary for the `530` complement-linker rule. In governed frames, `590` has `7` rows: `3/7` are terminal after `590`, only `4/7` are one-complement rows, and only `1/4` of those complements is closure-like. That sharply separates it from `530`, which has `4/4` one-complement rows and `4/4` closure-like complements. So `590` stays a bridge/formula branch, not a `530`-style complement linker.

Kill condition:

If the second `032` in `3335.1` is not real, or if the row direction/order is wrong, this bridge model dies. If more rows show `390-590-032-002-Y` or `Y-032-002-390-590-032`, the bridge model upgrades.

## Grammar-vs-Formula Discriminator

Promote grammar if:

- `235-002` branch-head cases keep selecting `125`.
- `002-390-X` continues to show high selector entropy across sites/registers.
- Source-visible H-1993, M-1825, Dholavira, or `3335.1` preserve their predicted tails.
- `390` under `002` keeps behaving differently from raw `390-590`.

Promote copied-formula adversary if:

- The same full-left and same image register explain the repeated `X` families.
- Source-visible rows collapse into repeated visual plates or duplicate catalog formulas.
- `125`, `095`, `692`, and `705` fail to predict anything outside their exact copied strings.

## Next Non-Provenance Tests

1. Hold out all `235-002` rows and score whether branch heads predict `125` better than closure heads.
2. Build a tiny classifier: closure head vs open head after `002`, using only tail entropy and terminality.
3. Test whether `390-590-032` rows share enough context to support `3335.1` as a formula bridge.
4. Compare `004-002-390-095` and `004-002-390-125` to see whether `004` is neutral while `X` comes from the branch environment.
5. Test whether `692` remains terminal under `002-390` or admits long continuations.

A new `297-350` row should continue to select `125-413`; if it does not, the pair-head bet takes direct damage. A new `610` row outside `002-610-125-032` damages the rare-head bet. A new governed `125` row should be scored by its specific `H+T` pairing, not by whether it merely ends the line.

Result:

| bet | result | confidence movement |
|---|---|---|
| `390` as open selector head | `15` frames, `0` terminal immediately after `390`, next entropy `3.106891`, top exact-text share `0.066667` | survives as candidate |

| `002` opens different branch types | closure heads: `861`, `817`, `820`, `000`, `824`, `880`; open heads: `031`, `390`, `368`, `220`, `595`, `297`, `365`, `374`, `900`; formula-locked heads: `405`, `056` | keeps `002` dependency-opener candidate alive |

Narrowed grammar:

`002` does not mean "then close." It opens a branch whose head class must be inferred:

```text
002 + closure_head      -> short closure branch
002 + formula_head      -> copied/register formula branch
002 + open_selector_head -> governed H-X selector phrase
```

`390` belongs in the third bucket for now. Its falsifier is no longer just "a nonterminal tail exists." The real falsifier is exact-text or one-tail domination. If new `002-390-X` rows make `390` look like `405-501`, demote it to formula-locked.

But it is not a hard terminal rule: the Mohenjo-daro square-seal exceptions force an exception lane, and they may define a separate register where `060-692` can be followed by another phrase. Cross-head transfer also blocks global `692 = close`: `002-455-692-416` is a non-`390` governed continuation.

Adversarial warning:

This is still canonical transcription collapse, not source-normalized signband collapse. The strongest rescue or kill now comes from the six continuing exceptions: if they are real same-line continuations, `060-692` is a terminal tendency with a seal-register exception; if they are joins/damage/side artifacts, the suffix becomes much cleaner.

Falsifier:

Source-normalized collapse of the cross-site terminal examples into a few copied/duplicate families demotes the suffix. Source-visible preservation of terminal `060-692` across Harappa, Mohenjo-daro, Banawali, Chanhu-daro, and Nausharo promotes it. A row showing `002-390-060-692`, or paired formula variants with and without `060`, would promote the stripped `390-692` bridge.

If source-visible `002-H-530` or `002-H-530-Y-Z` appears, the rule takes direct damage. If another branch with comparable count, head/site spread, and exact one-complement behavior emerges, `530` becomes one member of a broader one-complement class rather than a sign-specific rule.

## Consolidation Window 13:44: Minimal Parser v2

Phase: CONSOLIDATE, 2026-05-31 America/Los_Angeles.

Smallest parser that currently carries the evidence:

```text
002 = governed-frame cue
390 = local open-selector head
L   = left-context operator, scoped before X is read
X   = branch selector: either continuing/open, complement-linking, formula-bridge, or closed/terminal
```

Survivors after compression:

| survivor | tier | why it survives |
|---|---|---|
| target `002-390-X` row-level parser | candidate | `15/15` target forecasts have no internal violation |
| `002` as governed-frame cue | candidate | needed across the governed frames, but semantic value remains wild |
| `390` as local open-selector head | candidate | branch diversity survives raw inheritance test; not unique across the script |
| final `235` plus P086 head predicts `125` | candidate | `3/3` pass in P086, but exact-left shadow support is still sparse |
| `125` as refunctionalized continuing selector | candidate | post-`002` `390-125` is `4/4` continuing with zero raw tail overlap |
| `530` as one-complement linker | candidate syntax, closure semantics demoted | `4/4` one-complement survives, but complement closure semantics are too strong because H-773's `741` is a nonterminal governed head elsewhere |
| `590` as inherited local formula bridge | local candidate, global killed | behaves unlike `530`; retained only through target `002-390-590-032`; ordinary global `390-590` does not usually take `032` next |
| `095` as terminal closure | syntax candidate, status/admin wild | closed behavior survives, but strict `M-71` seal and route-only H-1993 tablet are too context-heterogeneous for status/admin semantics |
| `692` as terminal result/suffix branch | candidate-edge | strict `M-70` keeps a `032`-lane result/suffix read alive, but it is still a singleton semantic bet |
| `705` as terminal formula-ecology reuse | syntax candidate, semantics wild-to-candidate-edge | terminal behavior survives, but both repeated witnesses are still non-strict |
| final `004` as neutral qualifier | candidate-mixed | exact-left `390-004` group supports mixed branch behavior, not a direct selector |
| local/P086 `032` no-`235` suppresses `125` | candidate | works in target/P086 scope; global version is killed by `H-130` |

Killed or demoted now:

| claim | decision | reason |
|---|---|---|

| `390` rare open lane | candidate | only `2/47` `032-002` rows select `390`; both continue, with left contexts `205` and `226`; none after `220` | frequent terminal `032-002-390` rows or a `220-032-002-390-X` row |
| `590-032-002` tag pivot | wild shot | `3` rows, all Lothal tags; heads are `000:2` and `880:1` | source-visible seal rows where `590-032-002` selects open `390`-like tails |

Expansion output:

```text
032-002 frame rows: 47
closed core heads: 820, 861, 817
peripheral dirty closed head: 000
220-032-002 rows: 27
205-032-002 rows: 2
590-032-002 rows: 3
032-002-390 rows: 2
```

Interpretation:

This is the first decent left-conditioned parser bet for the `032-002` neighborhood. `032-002` does not mean "then `390`." It mostly routes into terminal or near-terminal classifier heads, especially after `220`. The open `390` lane is rare and appears under special left signs: strict M-70 has `226-032-002-390-692`; unbound `3335.1` has `205-032-002-390-590-032`.

That makes `3335.1` more important, not because of provenance hygiene, but because it is one of only two `032-002-390` rows and the only one carrying the formula payload. If it dies, the parser still has M-70, but the `205` payload-alternation lane gets demoted. If it binds, `205` becomes a real local environment that can select an open payload instead of the `220` terminal-classifier stream.

The `590-032-002` tag-pivot bet is intentionally wild. It says the reverse Lothal tag rows are not just noise: when `032` follows `390-590`, the following `002` routes into terminal tag heads (`000/880`) rather than the seal-side open `390` lane. This is useful because it can be embarrassed fast by a strict seal row with `590-032-002-390-X`.

Parser consequence:

```text
if row has P-032-002-H:
    if P == 220:
        bias H toward terminal classifier heads 817/820/861
        predict no open 390 lane unless a strict row proves it
    if P == 205:
        allow payload alternation between M-143-like 252-840 and 3335-like 390-590-032
        require source-bound 3335.1 before raising this above candidate-edge
    if P == 226 and H == 390:
        keep M-70 as the strict open-lane witness with terminal 692
    if P == 590:
        treat as possible Lothal/tag pivot, not a seal-side open-lane predictor
```

Next destructive work inside EXPAND:

1. Search specifically for `220-032-002-390`; one strict hit damages the terminal-environment candidate.
2. Search for productive continuations after `817/820/861`; repeated nonterminal tails demote the closed-head core.
3. Search for `590-032-002-390-X`; one strict seal-side row damages the Lothal tag-pivot split.

## Provisional Translation-System Rule

Use this as an ugly parser rule for the next campaign step:

```text
if row has 002-H-X:
    if local context is P-032-002-H:
        treat 032 as governed head-selection context, not a global closure value
        if P == 220:
            bias H toward terminal classifier heads 817/820/861
```

If a new row appears with `235-002-390-Y` or `235-002-405-Y` and `Y` is not `125` or a tail containing `125`, the promoted narrow selector rule takes direct damage.

If a new governed row has `125-632-032` under a third head, the portable compound-tail model upgrades again. If `125-632-032` collapses to duplicate plate/copying or direction error, the candidate sub-tail bet dies.

If held-out governed `125` rows keep clustering into repeated `H-(modifier)-125-tail` templates across sites and frequency-matched non-`125` controls stop matching the pattern, broad `125` can climb from wild shot back to candidate. If the repeated templates collapse to same-plate/copy families, noisy damaged rows, or ordinary null behavior, keep broad `125` demoted and parse only named carrier pairs. The immediate falsifier is now specific: find `610` outside `002-610-125-032`, find `297-350` with a non-`125-413` continuation, or find `125-413` spreading outside the exact pair-head template.

## Expand Run: `125-820` Terminal-Cap Bet

Report: `data/open_prototype/reports/campaign_032_002_861_125820_terminal_cap_test_20260531_summary.json`

Risky bet: `125-820` is a P086/governed title-continuation plus terminal-cap subtype, not free name text and not an `820` title value.

| observation | count | parse consequence |
|---|---:|---|
| global `820` occurrences | `244` | `820` is too broad for a clean title/rank value |
| terminal `820` occurrences | `185/244` | closure/cap reading is the default background |
| non-`125` previous sign before `820` | `242/244` | `820` is not dependent on `125` |
| non-`125` previous sign with terminal `820` | `183/242` | terminality is a property of `820`, not of `125-820` alone |
| `125-820` rows | `2` | M-41 and Sktd-1 only |
| terminal `125-820` rows | `2/2` | keep as terminal-cap subtype, not open continuation |
| governed `125-820` rows | `2/2` | both remain inside `002-H-125-820` lanes |
| governed heads for `125-820` | `390:1;405:1` | P086-family carrier pressure survives but stays edge-tier |
| direct `002-H-820` rows without `125` | `3` | damages any semantic title gloss for `820` |

Decision:

`125-820` stays alive only as **candidate-edge/source-thin**: `125` is the possible rank/title continuation slot and `820` is the closure/cap after it. This is narrower and less glamorous than "title suffix," but it is easier to break. A source-visible nonterminal `125-820-Y` cluster or repeated non-governed `125-820` kills cap status. A third source-visible governed `390/405/near-P086` row with terminal `125-820`, or strict unwrapped Sktd-1 evidence, promotes it from edge to candidate.

Within the same local register there are `292` `002` frames. Final `235` before `002` occurs `18` times:

```text
all final-235 -> 002 frames: after-head 125 = 3/18
final-235 -> 002-390 frames: after-head 125 = 2/2
non-235 -> 002-390 frames: after-head 125 = 1/6, with M-119 as the open 125 witness
```

Decisions:

| claim | result |
|---|---|
| broad `235` triggers `125` after `002` | killed: only `3/18` |
| narrow `235 + 002-390` predicts `125` | survives as source-mixed local pressure: `2/2` |
| `002-390-125` collapses entirely to `235` context | rejected because M-119 has `484-002-390-125` and continues |
| `235` has title/rank value | killed for this layer |

Updated compression: `235` is not a semantic value and not a general trigger. It is a local precursor that strengthens the `002-390-125` branch only when the head is `390`. The actual surviving parser is head-conditioned:

```text
local parser:
  002-390 is the frame
  X=125 is the continuing branch
  final 235 is a narrow precursor that can select/support X=125, not the source of X=125
```

Two-layer parser after strict collapse:

```text
strict core:
  X=125 -> open/continuing branch syntax
  X=095 -> singleton terminal branch syntax
  X=692 -> terminal branch syntax, but background-terminal-biased

source-thin shell:
  L=235 trigger, L=032 split, L=004 neutral split
  X=705 repeated terminal ecology
  X=530 one-complement linker
  X=590-032 formula bridge
  X=125-820 terminal cap
  semantic glosses: rank/title/status/result/transfer all below candidate unless source gates promote them
```

Next EXPAND tests that matter:

1. Find or falsify a third governed `125-632-032` head. Promotion requires another head or a source-clean non-235 target row; free spread outside governed `125` demotes it.
2. Attack `125-820` by source-binding M-41 and Sktd-1. Current destructive read: M-41 is visually compatible as a continuous source row but only on the `405`/P086 side; Sktd-1 remains wrapped/non-strict, so the pair cannot promote. If M-41 loses terminal `125-820` or Sktd-1 resolves against same-line terminal order, the subtype collapses back to wild pressure.
3. Search for nonterminal `125-820-Y`. One source-visible nonterminal cluster kills the terminal-cap parse.
4. Source-check the two nonterminal after-`125` `632-032` exceptions: M-119 `900-563` and damaged/slashed M-1188. If both are real same-line continuation, demote `632-032` from terminal compound to mixed compound.
5. Keep `125-195` dead unless a second independent `125-195` appears outside the current singleton lane.
6. Promote or kill repeated `705` by binding M-1825 and Dholavira; one failure keeps `705` as source-thin pressure, two strict terminal rows make it the best non-`125` repetition.
7. Force H-773 token strictness. If `530-741` fails, the one-complement linker is deleted from the compact parser.
8. Force `3335.1`. If it loses `590-032`, the bridge branch is deleted from the compact parser.
9. Force H-1993 image evidence. If it is not terminal `095`, the `004` neutral split loses its Harappa side and `095` returns to M-71-only syntax.
10. Promote strict core by adding any source-visible `002-390-X` row that obeys the open/terminal prediction. A strict terminal `125`, strict continuing `095/692`, or strict raw-tail-sharing `125` row is the fastest way to damage the core.
11. Widen or kill strict-core scope with one outside-register strict row: H-1993 would test Harappa TAB:B `095`, Dholavira/M-1825 would test non-core or source-thin `705`, H-773 would test Harappa `530`, and Sktd-1 would test Surkotada `125-820`. Until then, say "local Mohenjo-daro seal syntax," not script grammar.
12. In the next EXPAND phase, attack the one strong residual directly: predict that new same-register `002-390-125` rows should continue, while same-register non-frame `125` may terminate. One strict same-register terminal `002-390-125` row kills the current best parser.
13. Also attack the compact non-`125` half: source-bind M-213/M-232/M-708 or find a new same-register non-`125` row. If any strict local non-`125` branch continues, replace the binary parser with sign-specific subrules.
14. Attack the narrow `235 + 002-390` precursor: find or bind same-register `235-002-390-Y`. If any strict row has `Y != 125`, delete the precursor rule and leave only the raw `002-390-125` branch split.

| head-class grammar | candidate | useful unit is terminal-default vs branch-bearing head, not sign value | text-family/source collapse erases the head-class split |

```text
002 = valency-frame marker
H = head selected inside that frame
X = head-selected branch/complement

tail after 125 = dependent tail, not a translation
```

No phonetic value, language identity, sign meaning, or translation is claimed. This is a parser role bet for `002` that can be broken by specific rows.

EXPAND phase, 15:24: held-out predictor null:

Report: `data/open_prototype/reports/campaign_032_002_861_002390x_expand_heldout_predictor_null_20260531_summary.json`

This is the direct grammar-vs-register test for all `002-H-X` frames with a real X slot.

Task:

```text
leave one row out
predict whether X is open or terminal
use only one predictor family at a time
```

Results:

| predictor | coverage | accuracy | implication |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| X-slot sign | `247/316` | `0.765182` | X identity carries predictive load |
| head after `002` | `257/316` | `0.692607` | head matters, but less than X alone |
| immediate left sign before `002` | `271/316` | `0.667897` | left-context-only router is weak |
| broad visual scope | `275/316` | `0.694545` | copied-register null remains alive but weaker than X |
| scope + X | `137/316` | `0.824818` | register plus X is strong but lower coverage |
| scope + head | `136/316` | `0.808824` | register plus head is strong but lower coverage |
| H-X pair | `121/316` | `0.958678` | repeated H-X pairs behave like microgrammar rules or copied formulas |

New bets:

| bet | tier | claim | falsifier |
|---|---|---|---|
| `X_SIGN_PREDICTOR_BEATS_SCOPE_AND_LEFT_NULLS` | candidate | X sign predicts open/terminal behavior better than left context, head alone, or broad visual scope | source/family collapse makes scope or left context outperform X |
| `HEAD_X_PAIR_MICROGRAMMAR` | candidate | repeated H-X pairs are near-deterministic continuation rules | family collapse erases pair behavior |
| `VISUAL_SCOPE_NULL_STILL_LIVE` | candidate | scope-conditioned predictors are competitive enough that copied-register formulae remain a serious adversary | X/head pair remains strong after source/family collapse while scope drops |
| `LEFT_CONTEXT_ROUTER_WEAKENS` | candidate | immediate left sign before `002` is not enough to parse the construction | future rows sharing left sign but differing X stop splitting |

This is the first result that begins to look like a probabilistic parser:

```text
P(open | H-X pair) is high when the pair has repeats.
P(open | X) beats P(open | scope) at broad scope.
P(open | scope+X) is competitive, so register cannot be ignored.
```

The grammar hypothesis survives this first held-out null, but not cleanly enough to accept. The minimal honest read is: `002-H-X` contains an X-driven parser signal embedded inside register/formula ecology.

CONSOLIDATE phase, 15:30: smallest parser after expansion:

The expanded candidate set compresses to this minimal parser:

```text
If a row contains 002-H-X:
  1. Treat 002 as a valency/reconditioning frame.
  2. Predict X open/terminal first from repeated H-X pair if available.
  3. Otherwise predict from X-slot function class.
  4. Use scope/register as an adversarial correction, not as the whole model.

For H=390:
  390 selects a broader X-slot inventory.
  It does not yet receive a semantic value.

Current X-slot classes:
  125 = slot-conditioned dependent-tail operator
  095 = terminal class-label payload
  705 = terminal class-label payload, with possible 320-705-125 exception
  530 = open operator/extender, not central to 390 yet
  590 = unresolved open-extender wild shot
  692 = weak payload evidence; probably raw boundary/terminal pressure
```

Survivors:

| candidate | tier now | reason it survives |
|---|---|---|
| `SIGN002_PAIR_RECONDITIONING_OPERATOR` | candidate | `002` opens `390-125` but terminalizes zero-complement pairs like `031-000` |
| `X_SIGN_PREDICTOR_BEATS_SCOPE_AND_LEFT_NULLS` | candidate | X sign predicts held-out open/terminal better than broad scope, head-alone, or left-context |
| `HEAD_X_PAIR_MICROGRAMMAR` | candidate | repeated H-X pairs predict held-out behavior at `116/121`, but could still be formula-copying |
| `SIGN390_X_PAYLOAD_CLASSIFIER` | candidate, narrowed | `390` is a head selecting X inventory, not a standalone meaning |
| `X125_OPERATOR_SIGNAL_IS_TAIL_CONSTRAINT_NOT_OPEN_RATE` | candidate | X-slot `125` has constrained tails; open rate alone was not enough |
| `X095_X705_TERMINAL_CLASS_LABELS` | candidate | `095` terminal `3/3`; `705` terminal `4/5`; both cross site/register better than name-list behavior |
| `DHOLAVIRA_705_SHOULD_CLOSE_IF_BOUND` | candidate | Dholavira now adjudicates a parser prediction: `002-390-705` should close |

Killed or demoted:

| claim | decision | why |
|---|---|---|
| `125` means "continuation" | killed | non-X `125` can terminate, and X-slot `125` exceptions exist |
| universal `002-H-125 -> continuation` | killed | `002-861-125` and `002-906-125` are terminal |
| left-context-only router | demoted hard | left context predicts worse than X sign; same left contexts split by X |
| `390` assigns every X its meaning | demoted | `095/705/140/072` behave like broader X-slot classes, not 390-only values |
| `692` as strong `390` payload | demoted to wild/raw-boundary | raw `692` is already terminal-heavy |
| `590` open-extender | stays wild | mixed X-slot behavior; `002-390-590-032` is promising but singleton |
| source-only Dholavira route | not a result | useful only because it can break or support terminal `705` |

Main contradiction that remains:

```text
X-sign beats broad scope:
  X accuracy 0.765182 vs scope 0.694545

But scope+X is strong:
  scope+X accuracy 0.824818

So copied visual-register formulae are not dead.
The live parser is grammar-signal-inside-register-ecology, not clean grammar.
```

The next EXPAND phase must try to break these exact predictions:

1. Find or induce a strict terminal `002-390-125`. That kills the strongest `390-125` open-payload rule.
2. Find a continuing `002-390-095-Y` or `002-390-705-Y`. That kills terminal class-label behavior for `095/705`.
3. Find another `002-320-705` row. If it lacks final `125`, kill the `320-705-125` exception.
4. Find another `002-610-125` row. If its tail is not `032`, demote head `610` tail selection.
5. Collapse repeated H-X families just enough to test whether `116/121` pair accuracy is grammar or formula-copying.
6. Add any newly source-bound row to the held-out predictor and check whether X/H-X still beat broad scope.

No translation is claimed here. The meaningful advance is a provisional parser with attack surfaces: `002` frame, H-X pair, X function class, dependent tail, and scope correction.

CONSOLIDATE phase, 15:37: minimal parser hierarchy verdict:

Report: `data/open_prototype/reports/campaign_032_002_861_002390x_consolidate_minimal_parser_hierarchy_20260531_summary.json`

I forced the surviving parser into explicit held-out hierarchies:

```text
grammar-first:
  H-X pair if repeated
  else X sign if repeated
  else scope
  else global

scope-first null:
  scope+X if repeated
  else scope+pair if repeated
  else scope
  else X
  else global
```

Result:

| set | grammar-first | scope-first null | decision |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| raw `002-H-X` rows | `0.740506` | `0.718354` | grammar edge exists, but small |

| collapse by pair + tail globally | `0.658436` | `0.641975` | grammar edge tiny |
| collapse by pair globally | `0.640693` | `0.645022` | scope-first slightly wins |

Decision:

```text
minimal parser = candidate only
not promoted
```

Why:

```text
The parser is useful enough to guide bets.
It is not robust enough to outrank copied-register nulls under all collapses.
Any future promotion must beat the scope-first null on the same held-out/collapse split.
```

Practical rule change:

```text
Every future parse claim must report:
  grammar-first prediction
  scope-first-null prediction
  whether the claim survives pair/tail collapse
```

This is not infrastructure; it is a demotion criterion. It prevents the parser from promoting itself by memorizing the same formula families.

This is a language-family-style discriminator without naming a family yet:

```text
If this is compositional grammar:
  slot role changes sign behavior.

If this is copied visual/register formula:
  the same sign should behave similarly regardless of slot,
  or slot changes should collapse under source/register controls.
```

The next destructive test is obvious: compare this role-switch effect under same-site/same-object-form controls. If `125` only flips because head-slot rows are Harappa/Mohenjo-daro seal leftovers and X-slot rows are a different register, the grammar bet demotes.

Current parser, written as a rule:

```text
parse_002_390_X(row):
  require 002 before 390
  read 002 as FRAME
  read 390 as STATUS_TITLE_HEAD

  if X in {095, 705}:
    return "status/title closed by overt classifier X"  # wild shot

  if X == 125:
    return "status/title linked to complement after 125"  # candidate

  if X in {530, 590}:
    return "status/title with unresolved open/mixed tail"  # wild shot

  if X == 692:
    return "status/title closed by global edge sign"  # demoted wild shot

  else:
    return "underpowered classifier/residue"  # do not promote
```

Consolidation verdict:

```text
Promote to candidate:
  002 frame marker
  390 status/title head
  X polarity operator slot
  125 linker-complement role, wounded

Keep below candidate:
  095/705 lexical classifier meanings
  632/032/820 complement meanings
  any language-family discriminator

Accepted:
  nothing
```
